potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment rating s are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment

股價相對大盤走勢

萬海 (2615 TT)  Buy

近洋線進入需求旺季，給予投資建議為 Buy。  
投資建議 ：考量 4Q26 上旬美國線運價相對有撐，近洋線進入需
求旺季，目前 PBR 評價處於近年區間中下緣，給予萬海投資建
議為 Buy ，目標價 140 元。  
近洋線 4Q26 進入需求旺季 ：受惠中國十一長假前的拉貨需求，
颱風因素導致港口作業效率降低，加上巴拿馬運河限制通行船
舶艘數、船舶周轉率下滑等因素影響之下，支撐美國線運價持
續走高；歐洲線運價走勢相對疲弱，主因需求逐漸進入旺季尾
聲，加上部分航線復航紅海、潛在運力增加所致。美國線方面，
10/2026 需求仍相對有撐，隨後將進入傳統需求淡季，而農曆春
節前拉貨需求有望再度回溫。巴拿馬運河先前受到降雨不足影
響而緊縮通航條件， 10/15/2026 開始每日通航預約名額放寬，
通行等候天數增加進而降低美東線船舶周轉率，通航條件改善
後，將進一步減弱對美國線運價的支撐力道。近洋線方面， 4Q26
進入傳統需求旺季，運價有望延續 3Q26 漲勢。   
預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 19.84 元： 1Q26 處於需求淡季，加上市
場預期紅海復航導致運價低迷，萬海 1Q26 營收 336.41 億元，
毛利率由 4Q25 的 23.92% 下滑至 22.76% ，營 業 利 益QoQ-7.86% ，
另業外認列金融資產評價利益，單季稅後獲利 76.72 億元，稅後
EPS 2.73 元。 2Q26 受惠於需求回升、運價調漲，營收
QoQ+27.52% ，毛利率 30.37% ，營業利益 QoQ+91.12% ；業外認
列金融資產評價利益之下 ， 單季稅後獲利 115.35 億元 ， 稅後EPS 
4.11 元。 3Q26 單季營收 QoQ+38.96% ，主因遠洋線為傳統需求
旺季，適逢氣候異常導致亞洲港口塞港、以及巴拿馬運河限航
等因素疊加影響下，支撐美國線運價持續走高，近洋線同樣呈
現上漲趨勢所致 。 預估單季稅後獲利 229.78 億元 ， 稅後EPS 8.19
元。 4Q26 亞洲線進入需求旺季，價量有望齊揚；遠洋線進入需
求淡季，美國線運價走勢將較 3Q26 修正，預估單季稅後獲利
134.88 億元，稅後 EPS 4.81 元。整體而言， 2026 年供過於求壓
力較 2025 年舒緩之下，預估萬海 2026 年稅後獲利 556.72 億
元， YoY+76.94% ，稅後 EPS 19.84 元。

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 13 日

2 群益投顧 
  公司 ESG 政策：  
(一 )企業對於因應氣候變遷或溫室氣體管理之策略  
1.萬海秉持對環境保護的承諾，除符合 IMO 國際公約規範之下，進行船隊
減排，減少空汙，並且落實  設備保養及改善，確保機器良好運作，以達達
到環保之要求，愛護地球，永續經營。  2. 萬海積極參與國際環保平台，透
過實際行動來推行節能減碳的環保策略：  (1)2015 年參與 Clean Cargo 
Working Group (CCWG) 組織，加強與同業分享環保知識、資訊、技  術的交
流，以推動節能減碳為目的。自 2017 年起依據 CCWG 新規範，萬海提供
CCWG 的船隊相關航  行、油耗數據須由驗證單位檢驗，萬海船隊已連續五
年 (2016 ~ 2021 年度 )排放數據均通過第三方  驗證單位 DNV 之驗證，此將
可主動吸引同為其會員的貨主、承攬業選擇使用符合其環保政策之航商。
(2) 持續參與世界航運協會 (World Shipping Council,WSC) ，針對各項航運事
務及環境保護議題，  進行討論。萬海為該協會會員，副董事長為該協會之
理事會成員。  (3)2019 年加入零排放聯盟 (Getting to ZeroCoalition) ，萬海
持續推動並實現國際航運減少碳  排放為宗旨，致力達成該聯盟目標。  
(二 )企業溫室氣體排放量減量目標  
依照 IMO 規定，以 2008 年為基準降低碳排率，  以 2021 年為止減少 30%
碳排放率，並預計於 2030 年可  以達到 50% 減排率目標，優於 IMO 要求；
未來將持續進行船隊汰舊換新計畫，並配以新型節能主機及  各式最新節能
設備，以達節能減碳、環境保護目標。  
(三 )企業溫室氣體排放量減量之預算與計畫  
1.預算：保護地球本為每一個企業之責任，本公司將以提升船舶性能，降低
船隊油耗，使用低硫燃  油，以降低溫室氣體排放，視該成本為必要性支出。
2. 船舶能源效率管理計畫 (Ship Energy Efficiency Management Plan
(SEEMP) ：  依國際海事組織 (IMO) 之船舶能源效率管理計畫 (Ship Energy 
Efficiency ManagementPlan, SEEMP) 規範訂定有效的能源效率管理制度，並
根據能源效率設計指標 (EnergyEfficient Design Index, EEDI) 作為新船能源
效率的標準，並據實揭露能源效率營運指標 (Energy Efficient Operational
Index, EEOI) 。  3. 萬海另依 IMO 法規要求制定 SEEMP PART II ，加入「燃
油消耗數據收集計畫」 (DataCollection Plan, DCP) ，藉由執行燃油消耗數據
的監測，管理和報告船舶於海上航行時所產生的二氧化碳排  放，並依法規
製作年度排放報告，經第三方查核認證後提交給國際海事組織。  
4Q26 近洋線進入需求旺季：  
3Q26 以來遠洋線走勢呈現分化。受惠中國十一長假前的拉貨需求，颱風因
素導致港口作業效率降低，加上巴拿馬運河限制通行船舶艘數、船舶周轉率
下滑等因素疊加影響之下，支撐美國線運價持續走高；歐洲線運價走勢相對
疲弱，主因需求逐漸進入旺季尾聲，加上部分航線復航紅海、潛在運力增加
所致。  
美國線方面， 10/2026 需求仍相對有撐，隨後將進入傳統需求淡季，而農曆
春節前拉貨需求有望再度回溫。巴拿馬運河先前受到降雨不足影響而緊縮
通航條件， 10/15/2026 開始每日通航預約名額放寬至 33 艘 (正常為 36 艘 )，

3 群益投顧 
通行等候天數增加進而降低美東線船舶周轉率，通航條件改善後，將進一步
減弱對美國線運價的支撐力道。  
近洋線方面， 4Q26 進入傳統需求旺季，運價有望延續 3Q26 漲勢。  
圖一、 SCFI 指數

資料來源：群益投顧彙整  
燃油價格處於高檔震盪：  
油價方面，近期新加坡低硫油價格持續回落。美伊戰爭前約 500 美元 /噸，
戰爭後一度上漲至 1,120 美元 /噸，近期則來到近 890 美元 /噸。以目前運價
水準而言，已完全轉嫁燃油價格變動對營運成本造成的影響。  
新船下水規劃：  
公司至 2025 年底船隊達 116 艘，運力共計 57.27 萬 TEU。 2026 年預計交付
6 艘船舶，包括 2 艘 7,000TEU 及 4 艘 8,700TEU 船舶， 2027 年預計交付 1
艘 8,700TEU 及 10 艘 16,000TEU 船舶。  
營收比重方面， 2025 年美國線比重約 23% 、中東印度線比重約 27% 、亞洲
近洋線比重約 37% 、南美線比重約 13% 。  
預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 19.84 元 : 
1Q26 處於需求淡季，加上市場預期紅海復航導致運價低迷，萬海 1Q26 營
收 336.41 億元，毛利率由 4Q25 的 23.92% 下滑至 22.76% ，營業利益 QoQ-
7.86% ，另業外認列金融資產評價利益，單季稅後獲利 76.72 億元，稅後 EPS
2.73 元。  
2Q26 受惠於需求回升、運價調漲，營收 QoQ+27.52% ，毛利率 30.37% ，營
業利益 QoQ+91.12% ；業外認列金融資產評價利益之下，單季稅後獲利
115.35 億元，稅後 EPS 4.11 元。  
3Q26 單季營收 QoQ+38.96% ，主因遠洋線為傳統需求旺季，適逢氣候異常
導致亞洲港口塞港、以及巴拿馬運河限航等因素疊加影響下，支撐美國線運
價持續走高，近洋線同樣呈現上漲趨勢所致。預估單季稅後獲利 229.78 億
元，稅後 EPS 8.19 元。

4 群益投顧

元。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌，亦將出具降評報告。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

到 6-8 個月，載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑
戰 100 億元， 預計2027 年將新增產能 40%，以支應客戶未來需求。

載板 、 晶圓客戶 、OSAT 客戶均樂
觀，2027 年營收挑戰 100 億元

載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑戰 100 億元 
迅得 1H26 營收占比為 PCB 4 1%，載板 16%，晶圓製造 16%，封裝 25%，其
他 2%。PCB 主要產品以自動上下料、物件搬運及智慧倉儲為主； IC 載板亦為
自動上下料較多，整廠規畫約佔比 60%，單機搬運 20%，倉儲 15~20%；半導
體晶圓廠則以倉儲為主，台灣部分標準倉儲較多，微型倉儲較少， 海外部分目
前未出貨標準倉儲，僅供應微型倉儲，主要是安裝時間及人力配置考量；封裝
OSAT 廠主要供應倉儲佔比 60~70%，自動化搬運約 30%。

載板客戶積極擴產，載板三雄欣興、南電、景碩均為公司客戶，近年積極布局
轉戰載板市場的臻鼎亦為客戶，以欣興資本支出來看，2026 年 340 億元，2027
年長交期採購設備訂單金額 92 億元 ， 均將投入桃園楊梅廠及光復廠 ； 景碩2026
年資本支出 140 億元(原本是 80 億元)，將投入於六廠建置及設備導入，2027
年資本支出為 160 億元(原本是 100 億元)， 擴產進度明確 。 由於載板面積增大，
技術難度提升 ，為了維持相同生產效率須提高自動化資本支出 ，故以過往來看，
自動化設備佔客戶資本之約為 5%，現在提升至 15%。

晶圓製造部分，隨 客戶台灣及海外積極擴產，因目前現有自動化廠商均無單一
廠商可獨立供足客戶所需 自動化設備量體，故每個案件 公司均有相對應配比，
以海外廠來說，考量人力成本及安裝時間公司僅提供微型倉儲為主。 OSAT 部
分，客戶拉貨持續積極，半導體業務維持樂觀。以交期來看，過去 4~6 個月現
在已經拉長到 6-8 個月，載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑
戰 100 億元，預計 2027 年將新增產能 40%，以支應客戶未來需求。

維持買進，目標價 220 元

維持買進評等，目標價 220 元 
3Q26~2027 年營收逐季增長 ， 新產能逐步開出 ， 故維持買進 ， 預估2026 年 EPS 
8.51 元，2027 年 EPS 11.40 元(原估 10.95 元)， 目標價220 元。(2027 PER 20x)

風險提示 
出貨不如預期，ASP 不如預期。

TUẦN 28/09-02/10 [Điểm nhấn thị trường] 
VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tu ần qua, -49.02 điểm tương ứng -2.66%, với thanh 
khoản cải thiện

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
Xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn hiện vẫn đang được bảo lưu và VNINDEX đư ợc kỳ vọng 
sẽ có nhịp hồi phục trở lại khi về đến vùng hỗ trợ kế tiếp tại quanh 1710

### Page 2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -49.02 điểm tương ứng -2.66%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Y tế và Bất động sản là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -4,944 tỷ đồng, 
tập trung ở các mã VPB, TCB và VHM; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 1995 
tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng -5,280 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua và đang 
duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế 3,630 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 208 hợp đồng nhưng đang 
duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,428 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ hai liên tiếp sau nâng hạng, khi VN-Index lùi 
về đóng cửa 1.737 điểm — giảm khoảng 2% so với tuần trước và đang kiểm định lại vùng 
đáy cũ của tháng 8, quanh 1.720. Áp lực bán duy trì khi chỉ số giảm nhiều phiên liên tiếp, 
cho thấy hiệu ứng "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" sau nâng hạng vẫn chưa kết thúc, trong bối 
cảnh dòng tiền thận trọng và thanh khoản ở mức thấp. Khối ngoại tiếp tục là lực cản 
chính khi bán ròng mạnh cả 5 phiên trong tuần, riêng phiên 2/10 bán ròng tới 3.361 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở PNJ và HDB. Áp lực bên ngoài vẫn hiện diện khi Fed duy trì lập trường 
diều hâu và giá dầu ở mức cao; trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo KQKD sẽ bắt đầu có 
tác động, kỳ vọng theo chiều hướng tích cực, qua đó giúp nâng đỡ thị trường chung.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành cây nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên khung tuần, xuyên thủng vùng hỗ trợ 
gần quanh 1750, cho thấy áp lực bán đã có phần quyết liệt hơn sau 2 tuần giằng co trước 
đó. Trên khung ngày, sau các phiên lình xình vào đầu tuần, chỉ số cũng dần chuyển sang 
diễn biến giảm điểm rõ nét hơn về cuối tuần, đồng thời cắt sâu xuống dưới SMA50, thể 
hiện áp lực phân phối đang có phần lấn át mặc dù chưa xảy ra hiện tượng bán tháo khi 
khối lượng khớp lệnh được giữ ở mức thấp. Diễn biến tính từ vùng đỉnh 1870 vào đầu tháng 
9 đang không mấy khả quan, tuy nhiên xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn, tính từ đáy 1640 vào cuối 
tháng 7, hiện vẫn đang được bảo lưu và VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ có nhịp hồi phục trở lại 
khi về đến vùng hỗ trợ kế tiếp tại quanh 1710. 
Kháng cự xa: 1820 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1775 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1710 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1670 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu 
Mã Giá đóng cửa tuần Biến động từ đầu tháng Giá khuyến nghị mua Giá mục tiêu Lợi nhuận  kỳ vọng Điểm nhấn đầu tư Báo cáo phân tích

Năm 2026 dự kiến mở thêm 400-450 cửa hàng (+20%YoY) với DT trên mỗi cửa hàng cũ tăng 
trưởng 6%-8%/năm

chiếm 50% thị phần doanh thu ngành bán lẻ dược phẩm, từ mức 17%-20% hiện tại

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
September 14, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade:
Rapid advances in Artificial Intelligence (AI) have prompted prominent architects of
the new economy to conclude the Singularity is arriving ahead of schedule as signs
of its effects proliferate in the multitude of new AI models running in the increasing

number of data centers driving one of the biggest investment booms in U.S. history.
Superintelligence that surpasses collective human intelligence has long been one of the
predictions of futurists analyzing the implications of the Singularity. Superabundance is
one of the results of Superintelligence predicted by those who have written about the
consequences of this major acceleration in technological innovation. The early arrival of
these radical forces for change poses imminent challenges for society and investors that

will shape the next decade in critical ways.
In particular, the heightened uncertainty about how this new era plays out increases the
utility of a well-diversified approach to wealth management in our view.
Market View—Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite: The recent
rise in Treasury yields reflects a confluence of factors, including Federal Reserve (Fed)
policy uncertainty, deteriorating fiscal dynamics, resilient economic growth, persistent

inflation concerns and a record wave of debt issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate borrowers, particularly in the long end of the curve. Importantly, we view this
as a period of market adjustment rather than a cause for alarm.
Episodes of debt pressures can be a natural consequence of elevated supply, in addition
to other external factors and could be resolved through higher yields that help balance
supply and demand. While interest rate volatility may remain elevated and long-term

yields could settle at structurally higher levels, today's combination of attractive nominal
and real yields presents a potential opportunity for investors to extend beyond cash and
short-duration holdings and move closer to strategic duration targets.
The Ticker Tape—Top Questions Answered Directly by Our Chief Investment
Officer (CIO).
• What are the markets actually telling us?

2 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
While some academics have claimed the Singularity is still off in the distant future, or even
unlikely at all, those on the front lines of AI model development have recently asserted that it
has already begun. For example, Elon Musk has pointed to recent issues with AI models going

outside their proscribed limits, including hacking external systems and completing previously
unsolved math problems. Over a year ago, Sam Altman wrote, "We are past the event horizon; the
takeoff has started. Humanity is close to building superintelligence..." In short, the rapid evolution
of these models has convinced knowledgeable observers that the Singularity has begun.
Superintelligence is the primary driver of the Singularity as exponential advances in AI ultimately

create an artificial Superintelligence that is much smarter and faster than the collective
intelligence of humanity. As such, it can solve practical problems that remain unsolved by humans
and do it in a timely fashion. Superabundance is the logical implication of Superintelligence
as it works to create a world where economic goods, services and energy are essentially free
because of the omniscience implied by the most efficient processing of unlimited information by

superintelligent models. In essence, Superintelligence could mimic the Creation.
Some aspects of Superabundance include automated production of desired output that
eliminates most physical labor, clean energy and synthetic materials that eliminate resource
scarcity, and increased longevity as Superintelligence is applied to maintaining physical health
via, for example, synthetic organ replacement and personalized illness treatments. Obviously,
Superabundance pulls the rug out from under traditional economics, which is the study of how

scarce resources are allocated. While the "scarcity" vanishes in this new world, the allocation issue
remains and is likely to become the focus of political debate. In particular, the question of how
jobs and incomes fit into a superabundant world is likely to become a bigger and bigger issue.
The trend of technology replacing more and more of the labor share of income is already decades
old and dates back to the beginnings of digital technology. That trend has picked up speed

with the increasing profit share of output the new technologies created. The ratio of income
to the number of workers is orders of magnitude higher for the big new technology companies
compared to the old economy companies of the past. This means the owners of capital
(shareholders) are receiving a bigger share of the economic pie as high-margin, high-productivity
companies based in the new technology become a bigger part of the economy. This trend is

accelerating. That is a direct implication of the movement toward and through the Singularity.
It means the issue of income inequality, which has always been on society's radar screen, will
become bigger in the years ahead unless there are wise policies to share the superabundance
of the near future. Some countries are better prepared for this future than others. Norway,
for example, has built a sovereign wealth fund (SWF) over the past 25 years that represents a

substantial share of capital income for each of its citizens. In the U.S., with its widespread equity
culture, thanks to Individual Retirement Accounts and 401K plans, capital income is more widely
distributed compared to Europe with its much more debt-based capital markets. In any event,
widespread equity ownership is one way to deal with a superabundant economy where AI has
significantly reduced the labor share of income.
Demographics add to the natural evolution from labor income to capital income as societies

become wealthier. Aging societies of retirees by definition depend on capital income more than
labor income. This transition of the economy to higher margins and faster earnings growth is
the fortunate accompaniment to the need for more income from capital as societies become
older. The longevity trend naturally accelerates this process, which is the logical result of
technological progress raising productivity and living standards. People live longer in wealthy

societies, as subsistence constraints on life spans diminish while lifestyle and mental health
constraints become more the issue. How lifestyles and mental health evolve in a world where
work becomes less necessary is already showing signs of their increased importance for the
future of public health.
The current accelerating profits cycle reflects this impact of new technology on the relative
returns to labor and capital. The trend so far has been relatively slow compared to what's

coming if Superintelligence and Superabundance are the outcomes the Singularity promises as
increasingly seems likely. Because much financial analysis relies on past cycles the accelerating
Investment Implications

3 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
profits and rising margins of the current economy have naturally focused attention on what
happens when conditions revert to the mean of lower margins and more historically normal
profit growth. Looked at through this lens, Equities appear overvalued and destined to correct
bubble-like conditions. The Singularity, however, implies "this time is different," the four most
dangerous words in investing. Yet, the slowly evolving shift toward capital income already under

way is an early warning sign of bigger things to come as these trends are turbo-charged to warp
speed while the economy passes through the Singularity. Seen this way, a super-cycle in profits,
margins and stock values seems logical.
A revamping of the social safety net to address the conditions implied by Superabundance
is already a hot topic in Silicon Valley. Various proposals for a universal basic income (UBI)
are being bandied about. Typically, UBI refers to the government giving everyone an income

sufficient to meet some living standard. This automatically implies a big role for government in
the new economy. Given the fourth-turning breakdown in the political process and governance,
alternatives that avoid the government sector might also be considered. For example, another
implication of the Singularity is an accelerating accumulation of wealth fueling a growing supply
of altruistic and philanthropic potential that arguably could be administered more efficiently by

the private sector than the government where widespread allegations of corrupt administration
at all levels have proliferated since the pandemic. Given the growing wealth pie and the diversity
of social priorities among its owners addressing more social needs through philanthropy is a
likely option for the superabundant economy. In any event, the relative roles of government and
the private sector are necessarily an issue to be hashed out in this revolutionary time.

More glimmers of the UBI-like movements have popped up recently. As mentioned, Norway's
SWF is an example of how a well-administered government has been preparing for this future.
In the U.S. government sponsored tax-advantaged savings accounts from birth have been
instituted with private donations being added to the program. Governments, including the U.S.,
have increasingly been buying stakes in private investments for various purposes with the result

that the focus solely on sovereign debt can be a misleading indicator of fiscal fragility.
Japan is a significant example of the difference between gross government debt and net
government debt after subtracting government owned assets. Its gross debt is around 279% of
its gross domestic product (GDP) which is much higher than any other major economy. However,
Japan government assets total around 192% of GDP , including domestic and foreign Equities,

bonds, and loans. As a result, its net debt is less than 100% of GDP and more in line with other
major economies. As one of the most aged societies in the world, Japan points the way toward
how societies can cope with longevity. Government ownership of a broad array of higher yielding
global assets funded with low sovereign debt interest rates appears to be a path the U.S.
Treasury is also starting to pursue in managing its debt burden.
The main risks to the uptopian promise of Superabundance are the dystopian possibilities

that would follow from poor social decision-making. Around the world, different governments
will follow different approaches to navigating the unprecedented challenges posed by the
Singularity. As the U.S. celebrates its 250-year anniversary, it is in the midst of its fourth "fourth
turning." These are the intense political upheaval periods that demographers and historians have
documented in every fourth generation. The Revolutionary War, the Civil War, and the Great

Depression and World War II mark the first three "fourth turnings." The Singularity is a major
marker for the current fourth turning.
The relative success of the U.S. in transitioning through the previous major turning points in
history provides hope for a utopian rather than a dystopian outcome. Other countries have not
transited these challenges so well for such an extended period which helps explain how the
U.S. has become the most prosperous country on earth. Nevertheless, past performance is no

guarantee of future success. As always, each generation faces new challenges to overcome, or not.
For investors, the Singularity seems to be behind an unusually strong earnings cycle that could
potentially last much longer than past history would suggest. That's positive for Equities and
risk assets in general. The extra risks associated with this rapid transformation argue for well
diversified holdings as creative destruction on steroids seems a plausible outcome in a world of

warp speed change.

MARKET VIEW
Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite
Chief Investment Office, Fixed Income Strategy Team
For much of the past two decades, rising debt levels appeared to carry few
consequences. Federal deficits expanded and corporate borrowing increased, yet
Treasury yields generally trended lower and credit spreads stayed remarkably tame.
Today, the backdrop may be changing.

Federal borrowing needs continue to grow as deficit spending remains elevated. Net
interest expense has surpassed $1 trillion annually and now rivals total U.S. defense
spending. On this front there appears no end in sight as the Congressional Budget
Office projects that the fiscal 2026 shortfall will widen to roughly $2.1 trillion. Investors
are increasingly focused on not only the level of debt outstanding, but also the pace at
which new debt is being issued—with some questioning about the sustainability of this

situation over the longer term.
This phenomenon is not limited to Treasurys, as the issuance glut extends to corporate
debt. Investment-grade (IG) corporate issuance is running roughly 40% above last year
and is on pace for a record year. 1 While demand for Fixed Income remains healthy, the
sheer volume of new debt being brought to market is beginning to test investor capacity,
particularly at the long end of the Treasury curve and within certain sectors.

Hyperscalers Are Competing for Long-Duration Capital. A significant driver of
the surge in corporate issuance has been the hyperscalers. The largest technology
companies are financing an unprecedented wave of AI-related capital spending, leading
to nearly $280 billion of debt issuance year-to-date. 2 Many of these issuers carry high
credit ratings and have ramped up issuance of longer-maturity bonds. As a result, they
are increasingly competing with long-dated Treasurys for the same pool of institutional

capital, including insurance companies, pension funds, sovereign wealth funds and
duration-oriented Fixed Income investors—creating a crowding-out effect amongst high-
quality Fixed Income.
Fortunately, this is a case of pressure, not a credit crisis. Recent weakness in long-dated
Treasurys reflects a classic supply-and-demand imbalance and importantly is not a
solvency crisis for the U.S. government or high-quality hyperscalers. Investors are being

asked to absorb a growing volume of net new issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate America at the same time.
Debt Pressure and a Normalization, Not a Fiscal Reckoning. The rise in long-end
yields during August reflects this dynamic. The 30-year Treasury yield moved above
5.3%, reaching levels not seen since 2007. Fiscal concerns likely played a role, but we

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
believe heavy supply, sticky inflation, reduced Fed demand and increased competition for
investor capital were equally important contributors.
And importantly, the market's adjustment mechanism is already working. Higher Treasury
yields and somewhat wider credit spreads are attracting buyers and helping clear the growing
supply of bonds. This is a natural normalization and is how markets restore equilibrium.

Recent Treasury auctions continue to attract demand consistent with historical norms, and
investor appetite for new IG issuance has remained robust. These are not characteristics
typically associated with a loss of confidence in U.S., or corporate, creditworthiness.
A New Yield Regime. For Fixed Income investors, the more important implication
is that a 4% to 5% 10-year Treasury yield may represent the new normal. In an
environment of very strong underlying economic growth and unprecedented volumes of

debt issuance from both the public and private sectors, higher yields may be required to
attract sufficient capital amid a more competitive landscape for yield.
We continue to view both nominal and real yields as attractive and believe investors
should remain focused on achieving strategic duration targets. While rate volatility may
persist, today's yield levels provide a potential opportunity to seek attractive income that
has been largely absent for much of the past decade.

The Bigger Picture: Growth Still Matters More Than Debt Levels. None of this dismisses
the long-term fiscal challenges facing the U.S. Debt levels are rising, interest costs
continue to grow, and mandatory spending increasingly consumes a larger share of the
federal budget. However, history shows that debt levels alone do not determine interest
rates. Between 2007 and 2020, U.S. debt rose dramatically even as the 10-year Treasury
yield fell from roughly 5% to below 1%. What ultimately matters is the interplay between

short-term interest rates, debt levels, economic growth, inflation and investor demand.
For instance, nominal GDP growth and the 10-year yield tend to be correlated, outside
of recessions—we believe recent rate increases partly reflect this strong macro
backdrop. Moreover, economic growth is running above long-end yields, supporting the
sustainability of rising government debt.

The U.S. continues to benefit from several structural advantages: the dollar remains
the world's reserve currency, Treasurys remain the foundation of the global financial

system, and U.S. capital markets are still the deepest and most liquid in the world. Those
advantages continue to support demand for U.S. assets and underpin sovereign credit
quality even as borrowing needs grow.
The bottom line, in our view, is that even though the recent rise in long-end yields catches
many headlines, there is no need to panic. Instead, the recent repricing reflects periodic
episodes of debt pressure rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in U.S. credit. With

borrowing demand from both Washington and corporate America likely to remain elevated,
bond prices may continue to serve as the market's release valve. The consequence is likely
a world of structurally higher long-term yields, not a sovereign debt crisis.

THE TICKER TAPE
What are the markets actually telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investmen t Officer
When the markets speak loudly we should listen. There are often clues in the internal trends
within the markets that help us determine how far into an economic cycle we are or what drivers
of growth are most impactful. In addition, we can also find clues that help with determining

the micro picture. In other words, what industries are the most likely to benefit, from a
profitability standpoint is concerned, as the growth picture unfolds? Currently, the markets have
a megaphone, in our view. The year-to-date sharp outperformers have been consistent and
should continue to advance. These include commodities, including copper and oil, Emerging
Markets, Japan in the Developed Markets, Small-cap relative to Large-cap shares, Value versus

Growth, and more cyclically-oriented companies versus more defensive. This current consistent
outperformance informs us that we are still early- to mid-cycle in a supply-led expansion. We are
still early in the build out of the supply that is needed for the natural demand that is there. New
infrastructure is needed to handle the new economy that is progressing and quickly innovating.
This infrastructure needs commodities, all forms of energy, battery storage, minerals, metals,

recycled water, gases, chemicals, and all the compute and cloud capacity, including memory
and other components to handle the path toward mass tokenization. Moreover, the buildup of
government debt in the advanced economies as fiscal deficits widen is forcing large institutional
investors (including central banks and other government entities) to diversify their reserve
bases. This has increased the interest in gold holdings. Countries that have a vast amount of

natural resources, especially oil and gas, are in high demand by investors, which is obvious when
examining year-to-date outperformers. The bottom line is that the markets are telling us that we
are still early- to mid-cycle in this massive buildout, which will require a wide collection of hard
assets, Infrastructure (old and new), land, and within Equities, a more diversified approach to
exposure—particularly areas that are more cyclical- and Value-oriented. We expect this view to

extend itself well into the foreseeable future if not years.
In Fixed Income, it may seem more complex, but it is actually easier to understand what the
markets are telling us, in our opinion. The backup in
yields is “real yield” driven, which is more due
to higher nominal growth than to concerns of long-run inflation. This is evident in the Treasury
inflation protection market, break-evens in general and the ratio of Treasury yields relative to
other Go

vernment bond markets in the developed world. However, rising oil and gas prices and
inflation gauges that are still above the preferred level are usually the excuses for yields backing
up. Deficit concerns are a more plausible reason f
or yields rising but the main driver, in our view,
is still abo
ve average nominal growth. The yield curve and rates in general are still adjusting to
this new economic landscape that is very different than those of the most recent past.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bloomberg Commodity Index designed to provide liquid and diversified exposure to commodities via futures contracts. Is calculated on an excess return basis and reflects commodity
futures price movements.
MSCI Emerging Markets Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets countries. With 1,205 constituents, the index covers approximately 85% of the free
float adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI Japan Index is designed to measure the performance of the large and mid cap segments of the Japanese market.

MSCI Pacific ex Japan Index captures large and mid cap representation across 4 of 5 Developed Markets (DM) countries in the Pacific region (excluding Japan). With 93 constituents, the index
covers approximately 85% of the free float-adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI All-Country World Index is a premier benchmark representing global equity performance, covering large and mid-cap stocks across 23 developed and 24 emerging markets.

MSCI World Index ex U.S. captures large and mid cap representation across 22 of 23 Developed Markets countries--excluding the United States..
MSCI USA Momentum Index is based on MSCI USA Index, its parent index, which captures large and mid-cap stocks of the U.S. market. It is designed to reflect the performance of an equity
momentum strategy by emphasizing stocks with high price momentum, while maintaining reasonably high trading liquidity, investment capacity and moderate index turnover.

U.S. Large-cap Growth/Russell 1000 Growth Index measures the performance of the large-cap growth segment of the U.S. equity universe. It includes those Russell 1000 companies with
relatively higher price-to-book ratios, higher I/B/E/S forecast medium term (2 year) growth and higher sales per share historical growth (5 years).
U.S. Small-cap/Russell 2000 Index measures the performance of the small-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe.
MSCI USA High Dividend Yield Index is designed to reflect the performance of equities in the MSCI USA Index (excluding REIT s) with higher dividend income and quality characteristics than

average dividend yields that are both sustainable and persistent.
U.S. Mid Cap/Russell Midcap Index measures the performance of the mid-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe. The Russell Midcap Index is a subset of the Russell 1000® Index. It
includes approximately 800 of the smallest securities based on a combination of their market cap and current index membership.
MSCI USA Value Index captures large and mid-cap U.S. securities exhibiting overall value style characteristics.

MSCI USA Equal Weighted Index represents an alternative weighting scheme to its market cap weighted parent index, the MSCI USA Index. At each quarterly rebalance date, all index
constituents are weighted equally, effectively removing the influence of each constituent’s current price.
MSCI USA Minimum Volatility Index aims to reflect the performance characteristics of a minimum variance strategy applied to the large and mid-cap USA equity universe.

## Document 280: Cliff Notes_ searching for signal (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ searching for signal (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: searching for signal
By Sian Fenner, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
13:33 October 02 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, this week’s main event was the RBA Monetary Policy Board’s (MPB) decision to raise the
cash rate by 25bps to 4.60%. This outcome was universally expected given Governor Bullock had
already communicated that upside risks to inflation had materialised – the conditionality established by

the MPB back in August for considering another rate hike.

### Page 2

Governor Bullock made explicit note of three upside risks. The Middle East conflict was emphasised as
the risk that had “materialised”, given the re-escalation and renewed surge in crude oil and refined
product prices over recent months. The RBA’s liaison warns this could lead to further pass-through of
costs and more persistent inflation pressures. On top of this, domestic capacity pressures and the data
centre/AI investment boom were also noted as upside risks that the MPB remain alert to.

In a video update mid-week, Chief Economist Luci Ellis explains why the bar for a follow-up rate hike in
November is low. We now expect a 25bp increase in November, provided there is no sudden resolution
of the Middle East conflict beforehand, or some other event that significantly lowers the outlook for
energy-related costs.
Subsequently, the August CPI revealed that underlying (trimmed mean) inflation rose 0.2% (3.4%yr),
which was slightly below the market’s expectation but spot on Westpac’s forecast. As usual, the

monthly detail was mixed, but the key drivers of underlying inflation remain centred on housing-related
components, particularly rents and new dwelling purchases. Headline inflation remained volatile, with
the unwind of the fuel excise cut causing another spike in fuel prices. We have maintained our forecast
for a 0.9% lift in trimmed mean inflation in Q3, and with upside risks building for Q4, a follow-up rate
hike in November appears most likely.

Regarding the current growth pulse, household spending surprised with a flat reading in August.
Services spend fell back following recent strength that was driven by major sporting events, but this
was offset by an increase in goods spend, particularly in fuel and electric vehicles. Together with our
latest card data for early September, the consumer spending pulse is shading a touch softer. However,
another solid outcome for business investment could once again be on the cards for Q3. August’s

goods trade data showcased another significant spike in data centre-related equipment imports, up
$4.3bn, similar in scale to Q1. While clearly import-driven, our modelling suggests that the local
economic impact of such investment is likely to be around half of the total investment.
Before moving offshore, a final note on housing. Cotality’s latest update revealed that dwelling prices
fell another 1.1% nationally in September. While Sydney and Melbourne have led the correction to

date, the fact that the other capitals are also reporting price declines clearly indicates that the
correction is deepening and broadening, as higher interest rates collide with the tax policy changes
announced in May’s budget. Sentiment effects indeed remain at play, as evinced by the continued pull-
back in investor credit growth. Westpac anticipates a peak-to-trough decline of around 7%, with tight
on-market supply limiting the extent of the correction. For more, see our latest Housing Pulse.

Offshore, there was plenty of data to dissect.
In the US, inflation eased in August despite ongoing strength in household demand and higher energy
prices. Headline PCE inflation rose 0.3%mth and 3.4%yr, while core PCE increased 0.2%mth and
3.0%yr. Encouragingly for the FOMC, the share of PCE components rising by more than 3% over the
past year, a measure frequently cited by Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh, declined to 45% from 49%,
suggesting some easing in broad-based inflation pressures. At the same time, household spending

remained robust, increasing 0.9%mth, equivalent to around 0.6%mth in real terms. That said, personal
income growth was soft, rising 0.2% in the month, signalling households opted to draw down savings.

### Page 3

The ISM manufacturing survey was stronger than the headline suggested. While the index edged down
0.1pt to 54.5, manufacturing has now expanded for nine consecutive months. There were some signs
of capacity constraints emerging, with production softened in the month but backlogs up 4.6pts to 56.4,
suggesting that firms struggled to keep pace with demand. New orders rose 1.6pts to 55.3, primarily
due to domestic demand as new export orders eased, backlogs increased. Firms responded by lifting

employment, 1.5pts to 52.7. Against this backdrop manufacturers saw a renewed acceleration in cost
pressures, with the prices paid index jumping 6.8pts to 77.9. Firms cited higher steel and aluminium
prices, elevated energy costs, rising freight charges and ongoing semiconductor shortages, with every
major industry reporting higher input costs during the month.
The past week’s data will have done nothing to bridge the clear divide across the FOMC, as evident in

this week’s busy Fedspeak. Some participants argued for further rate hikes, citing strong inflation,
diminished supply capacity and policy that is not yet restrictive. Others counselled patience, pointing to
progress on inflation and that September’s hike buys time to assess upcoming data. The one thing
both agree on is that inflation will be the primary determinant of the timing and extent of further rate
hikes. Our view remains that one further hike is likely this cycle to bring policy closer to neutral.

In China, policymakers announced another round of targeted support measures aimed at stabilising
growth and supporting the struggling property sector. The PBOC lowered rates on its Pledged
Supplementary Lending facility by 25bps to 1.5% and expanded quotas for targeted lending programs.
Authorities also introduced subsidies for eligible first-home buyers. The subsidy applies to homes with
a floor area of 120m² or less and a value below CNY1.5mn, with support capped at 1ppt of mortgage

costs, up to CNY10,000 and for a maximum term of five years. The measures complement reforms
announced in August to improve developer financing conditions and support housing completion.
However, weak credit demand and confidence remains a significant constraint, suggesting policy
transmission is still limited.
The September PMI data pointed to a modest improvement in activity, although the details were less
convincing. Manufacturing returned to expansionary territory, rising 0.9pts to 50.1, supported by

stronger production and new orders. Much of the improvement, however, reflected a rebound in prices,
with the raw materials price index rising 7.6pts and producer prices increasing 6.2pts. Labour market
conditions remained weak, with the employment index falling a further 0.6pts and remaining below 50
for almost four years. Conditions in the non-manufacturing sector also improved, with the services PMI
lifting from 49.0 to 50.2, driven largely by stronger construction activity. Input and output prices moved

higher, selling prices returned back above 50, export orders improved to 48.5 and employment
increased 0.5pts, although at 45.9 it remains firmly in contractionary territory. Overall, the surveys
suggest activity is stabilising and policy support is gaining some traction, but weak labour demand,
subdued credit growth and soft domestic demand indicate the recovery remains fragile.
In Japan, the September Tankan survey delivered another constructive signal on the economy and

reinforced the case for further policy normalisation. Business conditions improved by 2pts for large
manufacturers and 5pts more broadly, while profitability indicators strengthened and capital
expenditure intentions remained elevated. Large manufacturers expect fixed investment to increase by
11.6% in FY2026, while large non-manufacturers expect investment growth of 11.2%, pointing to

### Page 4

Related articles
continued confidence in the medium-term outlook.
Capacity indicators improved further across both manufacturing and non-manufacturing sectors, with
measures of excess production capacity becoming less negative and indicating that demand remains
sufficiently strong to keep pressure on existing capacity. Labour shortages also intensified, with
employment conditions becoming more negative across most industries and firm sizes, pointing to a

progressively tighter labour market. Inflation expectations remained anchored around 2%, providing
further evidence that Japan is moving towards a more sustainable inflation environment. Overall, the
survey was consistent with an economy operating close to capacity and supports the view that a
December rate hike remains a realistic possibility.

## Document 302: Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Energy shock: Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022
10/2/2026 2
Will the same causes produce the same effects? In other words, will the war in Iran and the resulting
surge in oil and gas prices lead to an inflationary shock comparable to that seen in 2022? Will their
negative effects on growth be the same as those of the war in Ukraine and the subsequent energy
shock?
We have selected a set of indicators to track the impact of this new energy shock — caused by the war

in the Middle East — on activity and prices in the Eurozone, the United States, oil and gas markets and
emerging countries, and to see how much the current situation resembles that of 2022 at the outbreak
of the conflict in Ukraine.
This dashboard features charts and comments that will be updated on a monthly basis for as long as
necessary.
Overall, based on data available through September 2026, economic activity continues to show
resilience. The high levels of business climate surveys illustrate this well. Household confidence,

however, is more vulnerable and sensitive to energy price pressures. Financing conditions in emerging
economies also continue to hold up well against the shock (much better than in 2022) as well as
against the tensions in DM bond markets, which intensified this summer, partly in the wake of the
reinforcing upward pressure on energy prices. Higher energy prices are also fueling inflationary
pressures and pushing inflation even higher. In particular, Eurozone inflation rose by 0.6 percentage

points to 3.8% year-on-year (September flash estimate), reaching its highest level in three years.

to a 3-year high

## Document 315: France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 8 October 2026

EcoFlash

France: double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and 
stable non-performing loans ratio

Business creations rose by 12% y/y over the first eight months of 2026 1. This is significantly higher than the 5.3% y/y 
growth recorded in 2025. This increase was driven by sectors linked to information and communication technologies.

Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 5.3% y/y between January and July, according to data from the Banque de 
France (compared with the 3.6% y/y increase for the whole of 2025). The rise was more pronounced in the 1st quarter (+8% y/y) 
before slowing down. This slowdown could persist, as, according to Altares, payment delays have fallen to an average of 12.3 
days in the second quarter of 2026, compared with 14.1 days a year earlier (and 13.1 days for the European average).

Rising bankruptcies are more heavily affecting the services sector, which is also experiencing slightly stronger growth 
in business creations. This correlation is particularly noticeable in the retail sector, as business creations have risen by 13% 
since the start of 2026, while bankruptcies are up by 3%. In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been 
falling since the second quarter of 2026.

The average ratio of non -performing corporate loans has remained stable at a moderately high level for the past year  
(4.0% of total loans and advances in Q2 2026). In construction, where activity is more cyclical than in other sectors, the ra tio of 
non-performing loans has reached its highest level since 2019, standing at 9.5%.

The proportion of loans that have deteriorated but are not yet non-performing (“Stage 2” according to IFRS 9 terminology) 
is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all -time high (16.1%). This indicator, which helps to anticipate trends in 
non-performing loans, is relatively encouraging.

Despite a significant increase since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies remains, on average, below its peak in 
Q1 2025 (0.53 compared with 0.61, respectively). This indicator generally allows bankruptcies to be weighted based on the size 
of the businesses. However, the economic weight of bankrupted small businesses is at a historically high level (2.11).

Take a look at our EcoCharts on trends in business creations and bankruptcies in France and the 
associated risks. It will be updated on a quarterly basis.

Thomas Humblot 
Economiste, team: Banking economics 
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced economies, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

1 For more details, see Business creations and bankruptcies in France: double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, 
and stable non-performing loans, 8 October 2026.

## Document 319: FX Daily_ AI jitters have damaged USD safe haven appeal

After big moves in front-end USD rates, the dollar has shown it
could be a laggard in further stock market instability if led by AI
concerns. Still, we don’t think we are on the brink of a major
USD decline. EUR/USD may struggle to stay supported today as

Francesco Pesole and Frantisek Taborsky
Markets will be closely monitoring AI, France and Ukraine this week
USD: AI jitters leave USD on less solid
ground
AI-related jitters entered the FX picture last week, although
currencies are more accurately borrowing the reaction from the
rates channel. The USD two-year OIS/SOFR has dropped 25bp from
the 14 October post-US CPI print. These are mammoth moves
considering they have been driven almost entirely by AI concerns,
with eﬀectively no macro input.

### Page 2

Markets appear to have a clear reaction function in mind: stock
market turmoil is enough to drastically reduce Federal Reserve
tightening plans, even with inﬂation running hot. The reasoning is
probably linked to the market wealth eﬀect that many believe has
underpinned US economic resilience. This is enough to suggest the
dollar will not be the safe-haven of choice and may actually lag
many other G10 currencies if stock market instability originates in

tech/AI.
Still, contagion to broader US and world equity indices has so far
been contained, while the Nasdaq drop has only erased the last 30
days of gains. AI-related sentiment will remain an input for FX this
week, but could easily fade if tech stocks ﬁnd some stability. From a
macro perspective, despite the repricing lower in US rates, we do
not feel this marks the start of a major USD downtrend. A December
Fed hike remains a likely outcome; policymakers do not appear to

be in a position to send a dovish message at the October meeting,
and still-elevated energy prices argue against a material recovery
in global bond markets.
A return to 101.0 in DXY remains possible over the coming weeks,
but we do not see much more downside beyond that for now. The
US calendar is rather light this week, while the FOMC is in its pre-
meeting communication blackout. For now, we are not observing
any obvious FX impact from souring US-Ukraine (and Europe)

relationships. Markets are probably quite cautious about pricing in
an imminent end to the Russia-Ukraine conﬂict or any longer-term
implications for Europe. Things could change in the case of
meaningful developments this week.
Francesco Pesole
EUR: French protests may curb upside for
now
EUR/USD brieﬂy dropped back below 1.120 overnight before inching
higher again this morning. The move in short-term rate diﬀerentials

## Document 325: GLOBAL MARKET CALL_ Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLOBAL MARKET CALL_ Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-18
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight
Date:    3 hours ago
GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKET CALL:
Downgrading 'Go Global' To
Market Weight
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 18 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Robert Nyman / Unsplash
We are downgrading the “Go Global” portfolio strategy from overweight to
market weight and raising “Stay Home” (in the US) from underweight to
market weight.
Overseas earnings remain impressive, but the rallies in emerging market
economies’ stock markets have been led by just a handful of AI-related
stocks. Last week's rout in South Korea showed how vulnerable such
concentration leaves these stock markets. Additionally, European

economies look set to struggle as France's fiscal problems loom, and
China's economy depends almost entirely on exports. Plus, the Middle
East war could flare up again before or after the US midterm elections on
November 3, keeping oil prices, inflation, and interest rates elevated.
Here's more:
I. Global Bonds
The global bond rout took a breather last week. Yields fell in all eight
major bond markets we track, led by the US (chart). The US 10-year

Treasury yield fell 16bps to 5.08%, even as the CPI rose 3.7% y/y and the
PPI jumped 6.3% in September. That suggests bond investors see these
rates as attractive relative to their long-term inflation expectations.
The spread between French and German 10-year yields has stabilized at
around 137bps, near its recent high (chart). Spreads for Italy, Greece,

### Page 3

Spain, and Portugal have eased slightly from their late-September peaks.
Contagion from France has eased for now. But France's own fiscal risk
premium hasn't. The National Assembly began debating France's 2027
budget on October 13. Meanwhile, Paris is burning!
II. Global Stocks
Brazil leads the country ETF rankings mtd, up 12.3% in dollars, ahead of
next Sunday's presidential runoff (chart). South Korea is at the bottom,
down 17.7%. The US is up 0.1%, ahead of the All Country World ex-US

ETF, down 1.9%.

### Page 4

Europe's share of the All Country World MSCI's market cap has fallen to
13.0%, from as high as 30.8% in 2007 (chart). It looks set to keep losing
ground for a while. The US MSCI’s share has firmed to 64.8%, up from
62.4% in March. The Emerging Markets MSCI’s share has held at 12.1%
despite the Korean selloff.
The ratio of the US MSCI to the All Country World ex-US MSCI has turned
up in recent weeks (chart). It remains below its early-2025 high.

### Page 5

III. Earnings & Valuation
The All Country World ex US MSCI's forward earnings is up 41.4% y/y,
while forward revenues is up 11.0% (chart). The global economy is doing
well.
Overseas, earnings have far outpaced stock prices this year. The All
Country World ex-US MSCI is up 10.5% ytd, while its forward earnings is
up 35.9% (chart). Its forward P/E has dropped 18.9% ytd.

### Page 6

The US MSCI's forward P/E is 19.1 versus 12.2 for the All Country World
ex-US, a 57% premium (chart). US profit margins justify about half of it.
The US forward profit margin is 16.8%, about a third higher than the All
Country World ex-US's 12.7%.
IV. China Focus
China remains a source of deflation for the global economy through its
massive trade surplus. Exports jumped 36.3% y/y in September to a
record high (chart). The trade surplus was $133.5 billion. Semiconductor

exports more than doubled during the first nine months of the year.
Booming exports are the only reason China's economy isn't imploding.

### Page 7

The domestic economy remains weak, weighed down by a rapidly aging
population and a property debacle. The Shenzhen Real Estate Stock
Price Index is testing a floor that has held since 2012 (chart). We would
worry if it broke below that level.
The stock market hasn't benefited from the export boom either, so Beijing
appears to be propping it up again. Regulators met on Thursday to
discuss stabilization measures, according to Bloomberg. They reportedly

urged companies and state funds to buy shares and asked some
investors to curb selling. Mainland A-shares, where Beijing's state-backed
funds do most of their buying, held up into mid-2026 before turning down
(chart). Offshore Chinese stocks have been falling since late 2025.

### Page 8

clause.

## Document 327: How AI agents could reshape retail as stores move beyond sales

Source PDF: all report pdf/How AI agents could reshape retail as stores move beyond sales.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Agentic commerce, where autonomous AI agents
independently select and purchase products on behalf of
consumers, is forcing retailers to rethink their role. As AI takes
over more of the buying process, physical stores are evolving
from a sales channel into a destination for advice, service,
experience, and omnichannel support
Katinka Jongkind
A Galaxy General AI robot retail store, Shanghai. Retail is entering a new phase
of digital disruption, where agentic commerce could fundamentally change

how consumers shop
Agentic commerce fundamentally changes
the buying process
Retail is entering a new phase of digital disruption. E-commerce
has already transformed where consumers shop; agentic
commerce is set to change how they shop. Autonomous artiﬁcial
intelligence agents can search, compare and purchase products on
behalf of consumers, shifting shopping from an active task to an
increasingly automated background process.

### Page 2

Agentic commerce goes beyond AI
assistants and comparison websites
Key diﬀerences between agentic commerce, AI assistants (such as
ChatGPT and Gemini) and comparison websites
Source: ING Research
Most relevant for routine purchases, less so
for luxury goods
Agentic commerce is not equally suited to every product category.
Its greatest potential lies in repeat and replacement purchases, as
well as functional, standardised products. In these categories, AI

agents can eﬃciently compare prices, speciﬁcations, and
availability, while using consumption patterns to determine the
optimal time to buy.
Adoption is likely to be slower for luxury, trend-sensitive and
experience-driven products. In these categories, inspiration,
emotion, and personal preference remain central to the purchasing
decision, making consumers less inclined to relinquish control. AI
agents can assist by pre-selecting products that match predeﬁned

preferences, but ﬁnal purchasing decisions are likely to remain
ﬁrmly in the hands of the consumer.

### Page 3

Consumer electronics and personal care
lead the adoption of agentic commerce
Within non-food retail, agentic commerce appears particularly well
suited to consumer electronics and personal care. Both sectors are
characterised by a high degree of product standardisation and a
large share of repeat purchases, making them ideal candidates for
AI-driven purchasing.
In consumer electronics, purchasing decisions are often based on
objective and easily comparable criteria, including price, technical

speciﬁcations, and availability. This makes products such as
televisions, laptops and other electronic devices well suited to
automation, allowing AI agents to manage much of the purchasing
journey from product selection to the ﬁnal transaction.
The potential is equally signiﬁcant in personal care, where many
purchases are routine and repetitive. Products such as diapers,
shampoo, and toothpaste can be reordered automatically based
on usage patterns and preset preferences. By reducing the eﬀort

required to order everyday products, AI agents could further
accelerate the shift towards online shopping.
Agentic commerce is best suited to
consumer electronics and personal care
products
Product categories most and least suited to agentic commerce

Personal taste limits AI agents' potential in
fashion and living
For now, fashion and home furnishings appear less suited to fully
autonomous agentic commerce. Purchasing decisions in these
categories are often shaped by inspiration, aesthetics, personal
taste, and broader lifestyle considerations, making consumers less
willing to hand over control to an AI agent.
While AI can support product discovery and narrow down options

based on predeﬁned preferences, the ﬁnal purchase decision will
typically remain with the consumer. In these segments, AI is
therefore more likely to serve as a decision-support tool than as a
fully autonomous purchasing agent.
Chinese platforms lead adoption
Agentic commerce creates the greatest opportunities for large
global marketplaces and technology companies. These players
combine vast product assortments with the scale, data and
infrastructure needed to deploy AI agents eﬀectively. Their

integrated ecosystems, including payment solutions, logistics
networks, and data platforms, enable them to integrate AI agents
into their business operations faster and more eﬀectively.
Chinese platforms such as Alibaba and JD.com currently lead the
way, with AI agents already deeply integrated into their respective
platform networks. These agents can not only provide product

recommendations but also complete purchases autonomously.
Meanwhile, US technology companies including Amazon, Google,
Meta, OpenAI and Anthropic are investing heavily in the
development and rollout of AI agents, intensifying the global race
for leadership in this emerging market.
European players are structurally lagging behind their American
and Chinese competitors. Stricter regulation and less integrated
platform networks limit their ability to bundle scale, data and

services. While Zalando is among Europe’s frontrunners in AI-
powered retail, its applications are currently focused on product
discovery, comparison, and selection. Fully autonomous
purchasing has yet to become a reality.
Technology platforms are becoming the
gatekeepers of commerce
AI agents are rarely neutral actors. Embedded within commercial
ecosystems, they are inherently linked to the interests of platforms,
marketplaces and technology companies. Although agentic

commerce promises greater convenience and time savings for
consumers, it is also likely to strengthen the dominance of large
tech ﬁrms and marketplaces. These parties are becoming
increasingly inﬂuential in shaping product choices and controlling
consumer access.
Physical stores are not disappearing, but
their role is changing
How will the role of the physical store evolve in an AI-driven retail
landscape?

Redeﬁning the store in the age of AI
Agentic commerce is expected to accelerate the shift of sales to
online channels, putting further pressure on the existence of
physical stores. The extent of this impact will largely depend on
consumers’ willingness to delegate purchasing decisions to AI
agents.
Physical stores will nevertheless remain relevant for many non-food
retailers. Their role is shifting from sales channel to destination for

advice, service, inspiration, and support of online sales. Stores that
continue to rely mainly on transactional sales risk losing relevance
in an increasingly AI-driven retail landscape.
Retailers that successfully integrate their physical and digital
channels, deﬁne clear roles for their stores, and deploy technology
strategically can strengthen their position within the customer
journey. In a future shaped by AI-driven commerce, their role will

increasingly centre on adding value where automation cannot.

## Document 410: US MARKET CALL_ Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail

Source PDF: all report pdf/US MARKET CALL_ Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-17
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail
Date:    3 hours ago
US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is Too Big To Fail

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
US MARKET CALL: Yes, AI Is
Too Big To Fail
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 17 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Nils Rasmusson / Unsplash
I. Bonds
We've all been suffering from AI fatigue for a while. The daily news about it
has been head-spinning. We've been warned that AI is a bubble that will
burst, just as the late-1990s tech bubble led to the Tech Wreck in the early
2000s. Analogies to the credit excesses of the Great Financial Crisis have

### Page 3

also been proffered. Last week's rout in AI-related stocks convinced the
naysayers that the AI bubble is bursting, just as they’ve long predicted it
would.
Our happier spin is that the Bond Vigilantes are now doing a good job of
regulating and allocating capital to the AI ecosystem. Credit-quality
spreads are widening to reflect the riskiness of the bonds issued by some
of the dodgier AI-related borrowers like CoreWeave and Oracle. The
rapidly rising cost of credit for such borrowers should deflate the AI bubble

without bursting it. If some AI-related credits do blow up, the Godfathers of
the AI mafia (along with their protectors in the government) will clean up
the mess. As in "The Godfather II," Michael Corleone has too much at
stake to let his brother Fredo screw up the family business.
Meanwhile, the rout in the AI corporate bond market has triggered a flight
to quality and safety that has stopped the rout in the US Treasury bond
market (chart). This confirms our thesis that the recent rise in US bond

yields mostly reflects better-than-expected economic growth. Tougher
credit conditions in the AI corporate bond market should curb some of the
AI excesses. However, we doubt this will trigger an economy-wide credit
crunch or a recession. We aren't convinced that it will slow capital
spending much, since labor and materials shortages, along with public
NIMBY pushback, have already slowed the pace of data center
construction.
II. S&P Earnings

### Page 4

The big banks reported mixed but solid overall Q3 earnings last week. On
average, they met banking industry analysts' expectations. Last week, the
consensus EPS for all analysts covering S&P 500 companies edged up to
$365.70 this year, $424.19 next year, and $492.62 in 2028 (chart). Those
are extremely bullish expectations considering that we are still forecasting
$500 in 2030! If the analysts are right, the S&P 500 could rise to 10,000
by the end of next year, assuming a forward P/E of 20.0-21.0. For now, we

are sticking with 10,000 by the end of the Roaring 2020s, i.e., at the end
of 2029.
Last week, Q3's consensus EPS growth rate remained at 24.0% y/y, while
the Q4 estimate rose to 29.8% (chart). We've been among the most
bullish investment strategists on the Street, but analysts are still ahead of
us.

### Page 5

The S&P 500, S&P 400, and S&P 600 continued to rise to record highs
last week (chart).
III. Stock Valuation
The recent stock market selloff has been driven by falling forward P/Es
even as forward earnings have been rising to record highs (chart). The
former are nearing the troughs they made during last year's "Liberation
Day" lows.
Since roughly mid-2025, rising FEMO (fabulous earnings momentum) has
more than offset falling FOMO (fear of missing out) (chart). Since October

6, the reverse has been true: Falling FOMO is outpacing the rise in
FEMO.

### Page 6

IV. Performance
The stock market had a tough week last week. Leading the 1.95%
pullback was MAGS (down 3.89%), while XMAGS fell 1.38% (chart). The
Russell 2000 fell 2.4%, while the Nasdaq fell 4.5%. The DJIA rose 0.67%
for the week. The Impressive 493 is still outperforming the Magnificent-7
on a year-to-date basis, with a gain of 14.1% versus 10.2%.
V. Sentiment
The market letter writers have been more bullish than retail investors
lately (chart). Odds are they will both be a bit more pessimistic this week.

### Page 7

Only 47.4% of the S&P 500 are trading above their 200-day moving
averages (chart).
VI. Commodities
Aluminum prices are falling after matching their 2022 peak. This suggests
that supply-chain disruptions attributable to Gulf War III are receding, as
they had done after Russia invaded Ukraine (chart).

### Page 8

Copper prices remained near their recent record high last week despite
chatter that tightening credit conditions could slow the AI buildout and
weigh on overall GDP growth (chart).
The outlook for higher-for-longer diesel prices offers no relief (chart).

### Page 9

We aren't giving up on gold yet. The current correction resembles
previous ones that occurred in the middle of significant gold bull runs
(chart). This time, rising interest rates have been weighing on the metal's
price. However, significant buying by the People's Bank of China and
other central banks isn't interest-rate sensitive. We are still aiming for a
gold price of $5,000 an ounce by the end of this year or early next year.

## Document 478: グラフで見る景気予報 (2026年10月)

Source PDF: all report pdf/グラフで見る景気予報 (2026年10月).pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 23
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 2 日

調査レポート

グラフで見る景気予報 (10 月)

調査部 
【今月の景気判断】 
国内景気は、米国とイランの対立が逆風となっているものの、政府の物価高対策や代替調達の進展などが下
支えとなり、緩やかに持ち直している。内需においては、物価高を背景に家計の節約志向が根強いものの、雇
用・所得環境の改善が下支えとなり、個人消費は緩やかに持ち直している。他方、企業部門では、投資意欲は良
好なものの、設備や建設資材価格の上昇を受けて、設備投資は横ばいとなっている。外需については、日中関
係の悪化によりインバウンド需要の増勢が鈍化しているものの、トランプ関税の影響で落ち込んでいた米国向け
輸出が持ち直していることなどから、全体としては底堅く推移している。こうした動きを受けて、生産にも持ち直し
の動きがみられる。 
今後は、供給制約懸念の後退や良好な雇用・所得環境、堅調な企業業績を背景に、個人消費や設備投資と
いった内需が持ち直すことで、景気は緩やかな持ち直しを続けると見込まれる。世界的な AI 関連需要の堅調さ
に支えられて、輸出が底堅く推移することも下支えとなるだろう。 
ただし、景気の下振れリスクも依然として残っている。米国とイランの対立が長期化する中、原油・石油製品価
格の高止まりや円安進行によるインフレの再加速、原油やナフサといった輸入原材料不足による供給制約の再
燃には注意が必要である。また、日中関係の悪化に伴うインバウンド需要の低迷や戦略物資の輸出規制の強化、
米国の通商政策をめぐる不透明感、金利の予期せぬ上昇による住宅投資や設備投資の抑制、人手不足の深刻
化、地震・天候不順なども、景気の下押し要因として意識される。

【今月の景気予報】

### Page 5

1. 景気全般 ～緩やかに持ち直している 
2026 年 4～6 月期の実質 GDP 成長率（2 次速報）は前期比＋0.4%（年率換算＋1.4%）と 3 四半期連続のプ
ラス成長となった。イラン情勢の悪化によって物価高の加速や供給制約などに対する懸念が強まったが、政府の
対策が下支えとなり、外需を中心に底堅く推移した。今後も緩やかな回復基調が続くと期待されるが、米・イラン
の対立長期化に伴う原油価格の高止まりや供給制約懸念の再燃による下押しリスクには警戒が必要だろう。

### Page 6

9 月調査の日銀短観における大企業の業況判断 DI は、製造業では、イラン情勢の悪化を受けた供給制約懸
念が和らぐ中、コスト増加分の価格転嫁が進んだことから、石油・石炭製品などを中心に改善した。一方、非製造
業はコスト増加や地震・長雨などが下押し要因となり、宿泊・飲食サービスなどを中心に悪化した。先行きは、米
国とイランの対立長期化リスクが意識され、製造業、非製造業ともに悪化と、慎重な見通しとなった。

日銀短観（業況判断 DI）

（注１）シャドー部分は内閣府による景気後退期 
（注２）2004年、07年、10年、15年、18年、22年、24年、26年の各 3月調査時点で
調査対象企業の見直しが行われたため、前後の系列にギャップがある。 
（出所）日本銀行「全国企業短期経済観測調査」

4～6 月期の企業業績（金融業・保険業を除く）は、売上高が前期比＋2.7%と 4 四半期連続で増加し、利益率
も前期から改善したため、経常利益は同＋11.0%と 4 四半期連続で増加した（業種別の経常利益は、製造業で
は前期比＋12.1%、非製造業では同＋10.2%）。今後も米国とイランの対立長期化による資源価格の高止まりな
どが下押し要因となるものの、国内景気の持ち直しに支えられて底堅く推移するとみられる。

（注）金融業、保険業を除く

### Page 7

7 月の CI 一致指数は前月差＋1.7 ポイントと 2 カ月連続で上昇した。発表済み 9 系列のうち 5 系列がプラス
に寄与した。中でも投資財出荷指数（除輸送機械）や商業販売額（小売業）のプラス寄与が大きかった。8月のCI
一致指数は鉱工業用生産財出荷指数や耐久消費財出荷指数のマイナス寄与が大きく、3 カ月ぶりに下落すると
見込まれる。基調判断は「改善」が維持されよう。

景気動向指数（CI・DI）

（出所）内閣府「景気動向指数」

8 月の景気ウォッチャー調査の現状判断 DI（季節調整値）は 46.4 と 4 カ月連続で上昇した（前月差＋0.7 ポ
イント）。家計動向関連は、住宅価格や金利の先高観が一部で需要を喚起しているとの声を受けて住宅関連を中
心に改善した。企業動向関連は、好調な観光需要を受けて非製造業を中心に改善した。先行き判断DI（季節調
整値）は 5 カ月連続で上昇しており、イラン情勢が最悪期を脱出しつつあるとの見方がマインドを下支えしている。

景気ウォッチャー調査（季節調整値）

（注）景気の現状判断 DI は、家計動向関連業種、企業動向関連業種、雇用関連業種の景気ウォッチャ
ーによる景気判断から算出される。50 を上回ると「景気が良くなっている」、50 を下回ると「景気が
悪くなっている」という判断が優勢であることを示す。 
（出所）内閣府「景気ウォッチャー調査」

### Page 8

2. 生産 ～持ち直しの動き 
8 月の鉱工業生産は前月比－1.7%と 2 カ月連続で減少した。生産用機械工業などは増加した一方、自動車
工業や汎用・業務用機械工業などが減少し、全体を押し下げた。製造業の生産活動には持ち直しの動きがみら
れる。製造工業生産予測調査では 9 月は前月比＋3.2%（同調査をもとにした経済産業省の予測は同＋1.6%）、
10 月は同＋3.1%の計画だが、米・イランの対立長期化や相次ぐ自然災害の影響などで下振れる可能性がある。

鉱工業生産

### Page 9

8 月の鉱工業出荷は、自動車工業や輸送機械工業（除く自動車工業）などを中心に前月比－2.5%と 2 カ月ぶ
りに減少した。また、前年比では生産用機械工業などを中心に＋1.9%と 3 カ月連続で増加した。在庫は石油・石
炭製品工業や自動車工業などを中心に前月比－0.5%と 3 カ月ぶりに減少した。また、前年比では電気・情報通
信機械工業や金属製品工業などを中心に－1.9%と 19 カ月連続で減少した。

在庫循環図（左）および同概念図（右）

（注）26 年 3Q の在庫は 8 月の前年比、出荷は 7-8 月期の前年比 
           （出所）経済産業省「鉱工業指数」

7 月の第 3 次産業活動指数は、前月比＋0.4%と 2 カ月ぶりに上昇した。広義対個人サービスは上昇した一
方、広義対事業所サービスは低下した。具体的には、金融業・保険業などは低下したものの、生活娯楽関連サー
ビスや小売業などが上昇し、全体を押し上げた。均してみれば、緩やかに上昇している。今後も国内景気の持ち
直しに併せて上昇傾向で推移すると期待されるが、物価高などを受けて下振れるリスクもある。

### Page 10

3. 雇用 ～改善している 
8 月の完全失業率は 2.5%と低水準で推移した。就業者（前月差＋3 万人）の増加は小幅にとどまる一方で失
業者（同＋10 万人）が増加したが、労働市場への新規流入増で労働力人口（同＋11 万人）が増加したことが原
因であり、労働需給のタイトな状態に変化はない。人件費の増加などから中小企業を中心に採用が慎重化してい
る可能性があるが、それでも人手不足感は依然として強く、雇用情勢は良好な状態を維持しよう。

失業率と就業者数

（出所）総務省「労働力調査」

8 月の有効求人倍率は前月と同じ 1.18 倍となった。有効求人数（前月比＋0.7%）、有効求職者数（同＋0.7%）
とも小幅増加した。新規求人倍率は 2.06 倍に低下した。新規求人数（同－1.5%）が減少する一方で、新規求職
申込件数（同＋0.6%）が増加したことで、2021 年 11 月以来の水準まで低下した。人手不足は深刻化しているが、
賃金上昇を背景に企業は新規採用に慎重になっている可能性があり、有効求人倍率は緩やかに低下していこう。

有効求人倍率と新規求人倍率

### Page 11

4. 賃金 ～増加している 
7 月の一人当たり現金給与総額は前年比＋4.3%と堅調な増加が続いている。所定内給与（同＋3.8%）、所定
外給与（同＋4.5%）に加え、夏のボーナスを含む特別給与（同＋5.3 %）も堅調に増加した。また、足元の物価上
昇率鈍化もあり、実質賃金は同＋2.0%と 7 カ月連続でプラスとなった。雇用情勢の良好な状態を背景に今後も
賃金の増加が維持されるうえ、目先は政策効果で物価安定が予想され、実質賃金はプラス圏での推移が続こう。

現金給与総額と所定内・所定外給与

（注）現金給与総額、所定内給与は名目賃金指数から、所定外給与は月間現金給与額から計算  
（出所）厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」

5. 個人消費 ～緩やかに持ち直している 
7 月の二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は前月比＋0.5%と増加した。また日本の世帯全体の消費動向を表す 7
月の総消費動向指数（実質）は横ばい となった。基調としては、個人消費は緩やかに持ち直している。実質雇用
者報酬は引き続き緩やかな増加を続けているのに加え、消費者態度指数からみた消費者マインドは持ち直して
いることから、個人消費は当面、緩やかな持ち直しが続こう。

実質消費支出（二人以上の世帯）と総消費動向指数（実質）

8 月の商業販売額（小売業）は前年比＋2.7%と 6 カ月連続で増加した。内訳をみると、自動車小売業、各種商
品小売業、飲食料品小売業、医薬品・化粧品小売業などは増加した一方、織物・衣服・身の回り品小売業、機械
器具小売業、燃料小売業は減少した。季節調整値は前月比－1.2%と 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。9 月の新車販売台
数（乗用車、除く軽自動車）は前年比＋3.0%と増加した。

個人消費関連指標

（出所）日本自動車販売協会連合会「自動車国内販売」、経済産業省「商業動態統計」

8 月の消費者態度指数（二人以上の世帯、季節調整値）は前月差＋0.6 ポイントと 4 カ月連続で上昇した。内
訳をみると、「雇用環境」、「収入の増え方」は低下した一方、「暮らし向き」、「耐久消費財の買い時判断」は上昇
が続いた。消費者態度指数からみた消費者マインドは、このところ持ち直しており、良好な雇用・所得環境の下、
今後も持ち直しが続くと期待される。

6. 住宅投資 ～横ばい 
4～6 月期の住宅投資（実質 GDP ベース、2 次速報）は前期比－0.6%と 3 四半期ぶりに減少した。8 月の住
宅着工戸数は前年比＋6.1%と 5 カ月連続で増加した。季節調整済年率換算値では75.3 万戸（前月比－2.7%）
と 2 カ月連続で減少した。均してみれば横ばいとなっている。今後は金利上昇やイラン情勢悪化を受けた建設資
材価格の上昇などが下押し要因となる一方、所得環境の改善が下支えとなり、横ばい圏で推移すると見込まれる。

住宅着工

（出所）国土交通省「建築着工統計」

7. 設備投資 ～横ばい 
4～6 月期の設備投資（実質 GDP ベース、2 次速報）は前期比－0.9%と 2 四半期連続で減少した。設備投資
に先行する機械受注（船舶・電力を除く民需）は、7 月は前月比－3.7%と 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。うち製造業は前
月比－1.0%、非製造業（船舶・電力を除く）は同－2.6%といずれも減少した。このところ設備投資は横ばいとなっ
ているが、投資意欲は良好であることから、今後は持ち直しへ向かうと期待される。

機械受注と設備投資

### Page 14

8. 公共投資 ～横ばい圏で推移している 
4～6 月期の公共投資（実質 GDP ベース、2 次速報）は、前期比－0.5%と 2 四半期ぶりに減少したが、均して
みれば横ばい圏で推移している。公共投資の先行指標である公共工事請負額は、8 月は地方からが減少し、全
体では前年比－2.4%と 3 カ月ぶりに減少した。工事コストの増加を受けた公共投資デフレーターの上昇によって
実質値の伸びが抑制される状況が続いており、今後も公共投資は横ばい圏で推移する可能性がある。

公共投資と公共工事請負額

（注）国は独立行政法人などを含む、地方は都道府県、市区町村、地方公社の合計 
（出所）内閣府「四半期別 GDP 速報」、北海道建設業信用保証、東日本建設業保証、 
    西日本建設業保証「公共工事前払金保証統計」

9. 輸出入・国際収支 ～輸出、輸入ともに横ばい 
8 月の実質輸出は前月比－0.8%と 4 カ月ぶりに減少した。均してみれば、横ばいとなっている。好調な AI 関
連需要が下支えとなる中、イラン情勢悪化の影響が緩和へ向かうことで、持ち直しへ向かうと期待される。また、 8
月の実質輸入は前月比＋1.6%と 4 カ月連続で増加した。均してみれば、横ばいとなっている。当面、中東からの
輸入が伸び悩む一方、代替輸入の増加が見込まれることから、横ばい圏で推移する可能性がある。

10. 物価 ～企業物価は急上昇、消費者物価は緩やかに上昇 
8 月の国内企業物価は前年比＋ 7.6%と、電力・都市ガス・水道などを中心に前年比でプラスが続いている。   
一方、8 月の消費者物価は、「生鮮食品を除く総合」が前年比＋1.7 %、「生鮮食品及びエネルギーを除く総合」
が同＋1.9%と、緩やかな上昇が続いている。政府による種々の物価高対策により当面は安定した推移が見込ま
れるものの、原油価格の上昇が企業のコスト転嫁を通じて物価の上振れにつながる可能性がある。

国内物価

（出所）日本銀行「企業物価指数」、総務省「消費者物価指数月報」

11. 世界景気 ～米国は堅調、欧州も底堅いが、中国は弱い 
世界景気は米国は堅調で、欧州も底堅いが、中国は弱い動きが続く。4～6 月期の実質 GDP 成長率は、米国
は前期比年率＋2.2%に鈍化したが、個人消費、設備投資は底堅く推移し、民間最終需要は約 3 年ぶりの高い
伸びを記録した。欧州は同＋2.6%に加速し 4 年ぶりの高い伸びとなった。一方、中国は前年比＋4.3%に鈍化し
3 年半ぶりの低い伸びにとどまった。先行き、米国は底堅いものの欧州、中国は弱い動きが続く見込みである。

米国・欧州・中国の実質 GDP 成長率

### Page 18

12. 世界の物価 ～原油価格上昇の影響が懸念材料 
世界の消費者物価上昇率は、米国、欧州ではエネルギー価格などを中心に高水準で推移している。米国は 8
月に前年比＋3.4%と高水準で横ばいとなった。ユーロ圏は 8 月に同＋ 3.2%に加速した。 中国も 8 月に同           
＋0.8%と小幅に加速した。先行き、原油価格上昇の影響により、世界の消費者物価上昇率は一段と上昇する見
込みである。

米国・欧州・中国の消費者物価上昇率

（出所）米労働省、欧州連合統計局、中国国家統計局 
13. 原油 ～上昇後、やや下落 
9 月のブレント原油は上昇後、やや下落した。前半は、米・イランの攻撃の応酬が再燃したことを受けて、約 4
カ月ぶりの高値をつけた。後半は、「東西パイプライン」への攻撃などから落ち込んだサウジアラビアからの原油
供給が回復するとの期待などから、やや下落する場面があった。米国とイランとの交渉における駆け引きや、フー
シ派によるサウジアラビアへの攻撃は続いており、原油相場は中東情勢を材料に不安定な推移となろう。

原油価格の推移

### Page 19

14. 国内金利～上昇 
9 月の長期金利（新発 10 年国債利回り）は上昇。物価上昇圧力の高まりを背景に日銀の利上げペースが加速
するとの観測、高市政権の財政拡張による国債の需給悪化懸念の台頭、米金利の上昇などを受けて月初から長
期金利は上昇基調が続き、25 日には一時 3.115%と 1996 年 8 月以来、約 30 年ぶりの高水準を付けた。財政
悪化と物価上昇への警戒感、日銀の追加利上げ観測、米金利の上昇から、当面金利は上昇しやすい。

国内金利

（出所）日本銀行

15. 米国金利 ～上昇 
9 月の米長期金利は上昇。財務省が国債の買い戻し上限額を最大 60 億ドルに拡大すると発表したものの規
模が不十分との見方が広がったこと、原油価格が上昇しインフレ警戒感 が強まったことに加え、16 日の FOMC
（米連邦公開市場委員会）での利上げ後も金融引き締めが続くとの観測から上昇が続き、月末には 5.3%台と
2007 年以来約 19 年ぶりの高水準を付けた。追加利上げ観測を背景に金利が上昇しやすい地合いが続こう。

米国金利

（出所）日本経済新聞、FRB（米連邦準備制度理事会）

16. 国内株価～月末にかけて反発 
9 月の日経平均株価は下落後、月末にかけて反発。世界的な金利上昇、中東情勢悪化を受けた原油高、円
の反発、AI 開発を減速させるべきとの意見の米国での広がりを受けて、中旬にかけて値がさの AI・半導体関連
株を中心に下落した。ただし、シルバーウイーク中の米国市場での株高、原油価格の反落を受けて、月末にかけ
て反発した。AI・半導体関連株を中心に当面荒っぽい値動きが続こう。

日本株価

（出所）日本経済新聞

17. 米国株価～下落 
9 月の米国株価は下落。長期金利上昇から月初より軟調な推移となり、米 AI 開発企業のトップらが AI 開発の
減速を呼び掛けたこと、16 日の米連邦準備制度理事会（FRB）による利上げ、ウォーシュ議長が記者会見でイン
フレの根強さを強調したことなどが株価の重荷となった。米中首脳会談も特段の材料とならず、月末にかけても金
利上昇を受けて弱含んだ。金利上昇、物価高懸念を受けて、当面荒っぽい値動きが続こう。

米国株価

18. 為替～円は急騰後、反落 
9 月の円は対米ドルで急騰後、反落。円は月初に 1 ドル=160 円近辺まで弱含んだが、ベッセント米財務長官
による日銀に早期利上げを促す発言、日銀の利上げペースが加速するとの観測から、投機筋の円売りポジション
の解消の動きも加わって、8 日には 152 円 80 銭台まで上昇した。もっともその後は、原油価格上昇や米長期金
利上昇などを受けて円は下落に転じ、16 日の米国の利上げ、ウォーシュ FRB 議長の記者会見でのタカ派的な
発言を受け 156 円台まで下落した。さらに 18 日の日銀金融政策決定会合では、市場の予想通り 0.25%の利上
げが決定されたが、2 名の審議委員が反対するなど今後の追加利上げに不透明感が強まったことで円買い材料
とならず、連休明け後には米金利上昇を受けて 159 円台まで下落した。25 日に片山財務相が先の日米首脳会
談で「トランプ米大統領から円安への懸念が示された」と発言したことなどで円は一時 156 円台まで反発したが、
円買いの勢いは続かなかった。積極的な円買い材料に乏しく、原油価格の上昇や米金利の上昇が続けば、円は
さらに弱含む可能性がある。

19. 金融～マネーストックは緩やかに増加し、銀行貸出残高は堅調に増加 
日銀は 2026 年 9 月の金融政策決定会合において、基調的な物価上昇率が 2%の物価安定目標を超えて上
振れしていくリスクがあるとして、無担保コールレート（オーバーナイト物）の目標水準を 1.25%に引き上げた（今
次利上げ局面では 2024 年 7 月、2025 年 1 月、12 月、2026 年 6 月に続き 5 回目）。また、中東情勢悪化を受
けた原油価格上昇、AI 関連需要の増加に伴う半導体価格などの上昇、為替円安による耐久財などの価格上昇
により、物価の上昇ペースが速まることへの懸念を強めており、引き続き政策金利を引き上げる姿勢を示している。
金融を巡る動きとしては、8 月マネーストック（M2）は前年比＋2.0%と緩やかに増加している一方、8 月銀行貸出
残高は同＋5.8%と堅調に増加している。

【今月の景気判断】 
○レポート発表時における景気判断。【今月の景気予報】における各項目の現況をもとに総合的に判断。

【今月の景気予報】 
○3 カ月前～・・・3 カ月前～現況の「方向感」を、①改善、②横ばい、③悪化の 3 段階に分類。 
○現況 ・・・・・各項目の現在の「水準」を、①晴れ、②曇り、③雨の 3 段階に分類。 
○～3 カ月後・・・現況～3 カ月後の「方向感」を、①改善、②横ばい、③悪化の 3 段階に分類。

（注）現況をあらわす天気マークはあくまで「水準」を判断したもので、3 カ月前～の矢印が①改善、③悪化の場合で
も、必ずしも天気マークが変更されるわけではない。

【当面の注目材料】 
○各項目の現況、見通しをふまえて、今後景気を判断する上で注目すべきポイント。

【各項目のヘッドライン】 
○全 19 項目について、毎月簡単なヘッドラインを記載、当月と前月のものを対比して示している。 
○右端は各項目のグラフが掲載されているページ数。

【前月からの変更点】 
○景気予報において判断の変更があった場合に変更理由を記載。 
○ヘッドラインに記載されている市場データ（原油・金利・株価・為替）については記載せず。

【主要経済指標の推移】 
○当レポートで用いられている経済指標の半年間の動きを掲載。
